The answer should be D. Zinc.

Actually, let me reconsider one more time. The question says &quot;A metal-bridged homodimer is proposed&quot; and asks to &quot;base the assignment on protein-specific metal-binding evidence in its assembly context.&quot;

In Cu/Zn SOD homodimers:
- Copper (Cu) is the catalytic metal, coordinated by 4 His residues
- Zinc (Zn) is the structural metal, coordinated by 3 His + 1 Asp

The zinc ion plays a structural role in stabilizing the dimer. The Zn site is at the dimer interface and its coordination involves residues that are important for maintaining the dimer structure. In structural studies, the Zn ion is often described as being at the &quot;bridge&quot; between the two subunits.

Furthermore, the question specifically says to base the assignment on &quot;protein-specific metal-binding evidence in its assembly context, rather than on the mere presence of potential coordinating residues.&quot; This suggests we should look at which metal actually participates in the dimer bridging, not just which metals could theoretically bind based on sequence.

The answer is D. Zinc.
